{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_9ca401533ddf453b85e878dd","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Compression/Resume Solution Evaluation (bf-3k90)\n2\t\n3\t**Bead:** `bf-3k90`  \n4\t**Depends on:** `bf-2vke` (investigation)  \n5\t**Related:** `bf-17s0` (umbrella resolution task)\n6\t\n7\t## Problem Statement\n8\t\n9\tBased on investigation findings from `bf-2vke`, the core architectural conflict is:\n10\t\n11\t1. **Resume requires statelessness** — Same file + same streamId must produce identical block hashes\n12\t2. **Compression is non-deterministic** — CompressionStream offers no guarantee of reproducible output\n13\t3. **T4 privacy requirement** — Staging files MUST be wiped on completion/cancel/startup (E11)\n14\t\n15\tWhen compression is enabled, the sender's wire payload is the compressed staging file. If the sender restarts, E11 reaps the staging, and re-compression may produce different bytes → different block boundaries → different hashes → the receiver's persisted bitmap becomes silently invalid.\n16\t\n17\t## Solution Options\n18\t\n19\t### Option A: Preserve Staging Files Across Restarts\n20\t\n21\t**Approach:** Amend T4 to allow exceptional preservation of compressed staging files when resume is possible.\n22\t\n23\t#### Mechanism\n24\t\n25\t1. **Staging lifecycle modification:**\n26\t   - Staging files marked with `resumable` flag when created with compression\n27\t   - E11 reaping skips `resumable` staging files\n28\t   - T4 wiping deferred until session expires or explicitly abandoned\n29\t\n30\t2. **Session management:**\n31\t   - Sender tracks active sessions in persistent storage\n32\t   - On startup, sender reunites with preserved staging\n33\t   - User-visible \"abandoned transfers\" UI for cleanup\n34\t\n35\t3. **Privacy compromise:**\n36\t   - Staging persists across browser restarts\n37\t   - Multi-GB files may accumulate on device\n38\t   - T4a partially violated (privacy vs. functionality trade-off)\n39\t\n40\t#### Trade-offs\n41\t\n42\t| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n43\t|-----------|--------|-------|\n44\t| **Privacy (T4)** | ❌ Negative | Staging files persist, violating \"wipe on completion\" requirement |\n45\t| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Preserves compression state → resume works correctly |\n46\t| **Complexity** | ❌ Negative | Requires session tracking, abandonment detection, user cleanup UI |\n47\t| **UX** | ⚠️ Mixed | Resume works, but users manage \"abandoned transfer\" clutter |\n48\t| **Storage** | ❌ Negative | Multi-GB staging files accumulate on device |\n49\t\n50\t#### Implementation Complexity\n51\t\n52\t- **Medium-High** (~300-400 lines)\n53\t  - Modify E11 reaping logic to check `resumable` flag\n54\t  - Add session tracking to beacon protocol\n55\t  - Build user-facing \"abandoned transfers\" management UI\n56\t  - Implement staging expiry TTL (e.g., 7 days)\n57\t  - Add cleanup telemetry and monitoring\n58\t\n59\t#### Risks\n60\t\n61\t1. **Privacy regression:** Staging files persist longer than intended\n62\t2. **Storage exhaustion:** Users accumulate multi-GB files without realizing\n63\t3. **Cross-platform issues:** Staging files may not survive OS cleanup/browser cache clearing\n64\t4. **Edge cases:** Browser crashes, extension updates, OPFS quota limits\n65\t\n66\t---\n67\t\n68\t### Option B: Forbid Resume When Compression is Enabled\n69\t\n70\t**Approach:** Detect compression on the sender, signal via beacon, and fail-fast on resume attempts.\n71\t\n72\t#### Mechanism\n73\t\n74\t1. **Beacon flag protocol:**\n75\t   - `BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled` set when `PacketFlags.Compressed` is set\n76\t   - Receiver reads flag from beacon and suppresses resume UI\n77\t   - No resume state persisted (bitmap not saved to IndexedDB)\n78\t\n79\t2. **StreamId behavior:**\n80\t   - Unchanged: Same file → same streamId regardless of compression\n81\t   - Re-selecting same file after restart → fresh transfer (not a resume)\n82\t   - Different compressed session cannot resume from/to uncompressed session\n83\t\n84\t3. **User communication:**\n85\t   - UI shows \"Resume not available with compression enabled\"\n86\t   - Compression toggle shows trade-off: \"Speed (no resume) vs. Robustness (resume)\"\n87\t\n88\t#### Trade-offs\n89\t\n90\t| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n91\t|-----------|--------|-------|\n92\t| **Privacy (T4)** | ✅ Positive | No staging persistence → T4 fully preserved |\n93\t| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Explicitly disables unsafe resume → no silent corruption |\n94\t| **Complexity** | ✅ Positive | Minimal code change (~50-100 lines) |\n95\t| **UX** | ⚠️ Mixed | Clear trade-off: speed vs. robustness |\n96\t| **Storage** | ✅ Positive | No staging accumulation |\n97\t\n98\t#### Implementation Complexity\n99\t\n100\t- **Low** (~50-100 lines)\n101\t  - Add `BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled` enum value\n102\t  - Set flag in beacon when compression enabled\n103\t  - Check flag in receiver resume UI rendering\n104\t  - Update plan.md §8.3 documentation\n105\t\n106\t#### Risks\n107\t\n108\t1. **User confusion:** Users may not understand why resume is unavailable\n109\t2. **Transfer loss:** Multi-hour transfers restart from beginning on interruption\n110\t3. **Connection dependency:** Compression becomes viable only on stable connections\n111\t\n112\t---\n113\t\n114\t### Option C: Use Deterministic Compression\n115\t\n116\t**Approach:** Replace `CompressionStream` with a deterministic compressor that guarantees reproducible output.\n117\t\n118\t#### Mechanism\n119\t\n120\t1. **Custom WASM compressor:**\n121\t   - Integrate a deterministic gzip/zlib implementation (e.g., zlib-ng)\n122\t   - Fix compression level, strategy, and metadata\n123\t   - Strip timestamp, filename, and other variable fields\n124\t\n125\t2. **Determinism guarantees:**\n126\t   - Same input + same compressor version = identical output\n127\t   - Cross-platform consistency via WASM\n128\t   - Versioned compressor to detect algorithm changes\n129\t\n130\t3. **Fallback mechanism:**\n131\t   - If compressor version mismatch, abort resume\n132\t   - Beacon carries compressor version identifier\n133\t   - Receiver validates version before persisting resume state\n134\t\n135\t#### Trade-offs\n136\t\n137\t| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n138\t|-----------|--------|-------|\n139\t| **Privacy (T4)** | ✅ Positive | Staging still wiped; no persistence needed |\n140\t| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Deterministic compression → safe resume |\n141\t| **Complexity** | ❌ Negative | High complexity; custom compressor integration |\n142\t| **UX** | ✅ Positive | Resume works with compression (best of both) |\n143\t| **Storage** | ✅ Positive | No staging accumulation |\n144\t\n145\t#### Implementation Complexity\n146\t\n147\t- **High** (~800-1200 lines)\n148\t  - Integrate WASM compressor build pipeline\n149\t  - Implement compressor versioning and validation\n150\t  - Add compressor unit tests across platforms\n151\t  - Maintain custom WASM dependency\n152\t  - Handle fallback when compressor unavailable\n153\t\n154\t#### Risks\n155\t\n156\t1. **Maintenance burden:** Custom WASM compressor requires ongoing updates\n157\t2. **Build complexity:** Adds WASM compilation to build pipeline\n158\t3. **Browser compatibility:** WASM support varies (though broadly available)\n159\t4. **Performance:** WASM compressor may be slower than native `CompressionStream`\n160\t5. **Version skew:** Compressor updates invalidate all existing resume states\n161\t\n162\t---\n163\t\n164\t## Trade-off Summary\n165\t\n166\t| Option | Privacy (T4) | Correctness | Complexity | UX | Storage | Total |\n167\t|--------|--------------|-------------|------------|-----|---------|-------|\n168\t| **A: Preserve staging** | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ⚠️ | ❌ | 1/5 |\n169\t| **B: Forbid resume** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | ✅ | 4/5 |\n170\t| **C: Deterministic compression** | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | 4/5 |\n171\t\n172\t**Key:**\n173\t- ✅ = Positive impact (fully meets requirement)\n174\t- ⚠️ = Mixed impact (trade-off or caveat)\n175\t- ❌ = Negative impact (violates requirement or high cost)\n176\t\n177\t## Recommendation\n178\t\n179\t### Chosen Option: **Option B — Forbid Resume When Compression is Enabled**\n180\t\n181\t**Rationale:**\n182\t\n183\t1. **Privacy preservation (T4 compliance):** No staging persistence → privacy posture unchanged\n184\t2. **Correctness guarantee:** Explicitly disabling unsafe resume prevents silent corruption\n185\t3. **Low implementation complexity:** ~50-100 lines vs. 300-1200 lines for alternatives\n186\t4. **Clear user contract:** Speed vs. robustness trade-off is transparent\n187\t5. **No new maintenance burden:** No custom compressor or session tracking\n188\t\n189\t**Why not Option A (preserve staging)?**\n190\t- Violates T4 privacy requirement (staging must be wiped)\n191\t- Adds significant complexity (session tracking, abandonment UI)\n192\t- Storage exhaustion risk on multi-GB files\n193\t- Cross-platform stability issues remain (staging may not survive OS cleanup)\n194\t\n195\t**Why not Option C (deterministic compression)?**\n196\t- High implementation and maintenance complexity\n197\t- Custom WASM compressor dependency\n198\t- Build pipeline complexity\n199\t- Versioning issues (compressor updates invalidate resume states)\n200\t- `CompressionStream` is fast and well-integrated — replacing it is costly\n201\t\n202\t**Why Option B is acceptable:**\n203\t- Users choose between two valid modes:\n204\t  - **Compression ON:** 3-10× faster transfers, no resume (stable connections)\n205\t  - **Compression OFF:** Slower transfers, resume supported (unstable connections)\n206\t- The trade-off is explicit and understandable\n207\t- Implementation is trivial and low-risk\n208\t- Privacy and correctness are fully preserved\n209\t\n210\t## Implementation Outline\n211\t\n212\t### Phase 1: Beacon Protocol (Priority: P0)\n213\t\n214\t1. **Add `ResumeDisabled` flag to beacon**\n215\t   ```typescript\n216\t   // src/core/frame/beacon.ts\n217\t   export enum BeaconFlags {\n218\t     None = 0,\n219\t     Compressed = 1 << 0,\n220\t     ResumeDisabled = 1 << 1,  // NEW\n221\t   }\n222\t   ```\n223\t\n224\t2. **Set flag when compression enabled**\n225\t   ```typescript\n226\t   // Sender beacon construction\n227\t   if (compressionEnabled) {\n228\t     flags |= BeaconFlags.Compressed | BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled;\n229\t   }\n230\t   ```\n231\t\n232\t3. **Update plan.md §8.3** to document restriction explicitly\n233\t\n234\t### Phase 2: Receiver Resume Logic (Priority: P0)\n235\t\n236\t1. **Check flag before persisting resume state**\n237\t   ```typescript\n238\t   // Receiver beacon handler\n239\t   if ((beacon.flags & BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled) !== 0) {\n240\t     // Do NOT persist bitmap or metadata\n241\t     showResumeDisabledMessage();\n242\t     return;\n243\t   }\n244\t   ```\n245\t\n246\t2. **Suppress resume UI when flag present**\n247\t   ```typescript\n248\t   // UI rendering\n249\t   const canResume = !isResumeDisabled(beacon.flags) && hasPersistedState();\n250\t   ```\n251\t\n252\t### Phase 3: User Communication (Priority: P1)\n253\t\n254\t1. **Add clear messaging:**\n255\t   - \"Resume is not available when compression is enabled\"\n256\t   - \"To enable resume, disable compression in settings\"\n257\t\n258\t2. **Compression toggle tooltip:**\n259\t   - \"Compression: Faster transfers (3-10×), but cannot resume if interrupted\"\n260\t   - \"No compression: Slower transfers, but can resume from last completed block\"\n261\t\n262\t### Phase 4: Testing (Priority: P0)\n263\t\n264\t1. **Unit tests:**\n265\t   - Test beacon flag setting when compression enabled\n266\t   - Test receiver resume suppression when flag present\n267\t\n268\t2. **Integration tests:**\n269\t   - Compressed transfer interrupted → reload → resume UI NOT shown\n270\t   - Uncompressed transfer interrupted → reload → resume UI shown\n271\t   - Beacon flag validation end-to-end\n272\t\n273\t3. **Property tests:**\n274\t   - For any file, compression produces deterministic blocks within ONE session\n275\t   - For any file, re-compression MAY produce different blocks across sessions\n276\t\n277\t### Phase 5: Documentation (Priority: P1)\n278\t\n279\t1. **Update plan.md:**\n280\t   - §8.3: Explicitly document \"Resume unsupported when compression enabled\"\n281\t   - §12 (T4): Confirm staging lifecycle unchanged\n282\t   - §10 Compression/Resume Solution Evaluation (bf-3k90)\n\n**Bead:** `bf-3k90`  \n**Depends on:** `bf-2vke` (investigation)  \n**Related:** `bf-17s0` (umbrella resolution task)\n\n## Problem Statement\n\nBased on investigation findings from `bf-2vke`, the core architectural conflict is:\n\n1. **Resume requires statelessness** — Same file + same streamId must produce identical block hashes\n2. **Compression is non-deterministic** — CompressionStream offers no guarantee of reproducible output\n3. **T4 privacy requirement** — Staging files MUST be wiped on completion/cancel/startup (E11)\n\nWhen compression is enabled, the sender's wire payload is the compressed staging file. If the sender restarts, E11 reaps the staging, and re-compression may produce different bytes → different block boundaries → different hashes → the receiver's persisted bitmap becomes silently invalid.\n\n## Solution Options\n\n### Option A: Preserve Staging Files Across Restarts\n\n**Approach:** Amend T4 to allow exceptional preservation of compressed staging files when resume is possible.\n\n#### Mechanism\n\n1. **Staging lifecycle modification:**\n   - Staging files marked with `resumable` flag when created with compression\n   - E11 reaping skips `resumable` staging files\n   - T4 wiping deferred until session expires or explicitly abandoned\n\n2. **Session management:**\n   - Sender tracks active sessions in persistent storage\n   - On startup, sender reunites with preserved staging\n   - User-visible \"abandoned transfers\" UI for cleanup\n\n3. **Privacy compromise:**\n   - Staging persists across browser restarts\n   - Multi-GB files may accumulate on device\n   - T4a partially violated (privacy vs. functionality trade-off)\n\n#### Trade-offs\n\n| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n|-----------|--------|-------|\n| **Privacy (T4)** | ❌ Negative | Staging files persist, violating \"wipe on completion\" requirement |\n| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Preserves compression state → resume works correctly |\n| **Complexity** | ❌ Negative | Requires session tracking, abandonment detection, user cleanup UI |\n| **UX** | ⚠️ Mixed | Resume works, but users manage \"abandoned transfer\" clutter |\n| **Storage** | ❌ Negative | Multi-GB staging files accumulate on device |\n\n#### Implementation Complexity\n\n- **Medium-High** (~300-400 lines)\n  - Modify E11 reaping logic to check `resumable` flag\n  - Add session tracking to beacon protocol\n  - Build user-facing \"abandoned transfers\" management UI\n  - Implement staging expiry TTL (e.g., 7 days)\n  - Add cleanup telemetry and monitoring\n\n#### Risks\n\n1. **Privacy regression:** Staging files persist longer than intended\n2. **Storage exhaustion:** Users accumulate multi-GB files without realizing\n3. **Cross-platform issues:** Staging files may not survive OS cleanup/browser cache clearing\n4. **Edge cases:** Browser crashes, extension updates, OPFS quota limits\n\n---\n\n### Option B: Forbid Resume When Compression is Enabled\n\n**Approach:** Detect compression on the sender, signal via beacon, and fail-fast on resume attempts.\n\n#### Mechanism\n\n1. **Beacon flag protocol:**\n   - `BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled` set when `PacketFlags.Compressed` is set\n   - Receiver reads flag from beacon and suppresses resume UI\n   - No resume state persisted (bitmap not saved to IndexedDB)\n\n2. **StreamId behavior:**\n   - Unchanged: Same file → same streamId regardless of compression\n   - Re-selecting same file after restart → fresh transfer (not a resume)\n   - Different compressed session cannot resume from/to uncompressed session\n\n3. **User communication:**\n   - UI shows \"Resume not available with compression enabled\"\n   - Compression toggle shows trade-off: \"Speed (no resume) vs. Robustness (resume)\"\n\n#### Trade-offs\n\n| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n|-----------|--------|-------|\n| **Privacy (T4)** | ✅ Positive | No staging persistence → T4 fully preserved |\n| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Explicitly disables unsafe resume → no silent corruption |\n| **Complexity** | ✅ Positive | Minimal code change (~50-100 lines) |\n| **UX** | ⚠️ Mixed | Clear trade-off: speed vs. robustness |\n| **Storage** | ✅ Positive | No staging accumulation |\n\n#### Implementation Complexity\n\n- **Low** (~50-100 lines)\n  - Add `BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled` enum value\n  - Set flag in beacon when compression enabled\n  - Check flag in receiver resume UI rendering\n  - Update plan.md §8.3 documentation\n\n#### Risks\n\n1. **User confusion:** Users may not understand why resume is unavailable\n2. **Transfer loss:** Multi-hour transfers restart from beginning on interruption\n3. **Connection dependency:** Compression becomes viable only on stable connections\n\n---\n\n### Option C: Use Deterministic Compression\n\n**Approach:** Replace `CompressionStream` with a deterministic compressor that guarantees reproducible output.\n\n#### Mechanism\n\n1. **Custom WASM compressor:**\n   - Integrate a deterministic gzip/zlib implementation (e.g., zlib-ng)\n   - Fix compression level, strategy, and metadata\n   - Strip timestamp, filename, and other variable fields\n\n2. **Determinism guarantees:**\n   - Same input + same compressor version = identical output\n   - Cross-platform consistency via WASM\n   - Versioned compressor to detect algorithm changes\n\n3. **Fallback mechanism:**\n   - If compressor version mismatch, abort resume\n   - Beacon carries compressor version identifier\n   - Receiver validates version before persisting resume state\n\n#### Trade-offs\n\n| Dimension | Impact | Notes |\n|-----------|--------|-------|\n| **Privacy (T4)** | ✅ Positive | Staging still wiped; no persistence needed |\n| **Correctness** | ✅ Positive | Deterministic compression → safe resume |\n| **Complexity** | ❌ Negative | High complexity; custom compressor integration |\n| **UX** | ✅ Positive | Resume works with compression (best of both) |\n| **Storage** | ✅ Positive | No staging accumulation |\n\n#### Implementation Complexity\n\n- **High** (~800-1200 lines)\n  - Integrate WASM compressor build pipeline\n  - Implement compressor versioning and validation\n  - Add compressor unit tests across platforms\n  - Maintain custom WASM dependency\n  - Handle fallback when compressor unavailable\n\n#### Risks\n\n1. **Maintenance burden:** Custom WASM compressor requires ongoing updates\n2. **Build complexity:** Adds WASM compilation to build pipeline\n3. **Browser compatibility:** WASM support varies (though broadly available)\n4. **Performance:** WASM compressor may be slower than native `CompressionStream`\n5. **Version skew:** Compressor updates invalidate all existing resume states\n\n---\n\n## Trade-off Summary\n\n| Option | Privacy (T4) | Correctness | Complexity | UX | Storage | Total |\n|--------|--------------|-------------|------------|-----|---------|-------|\n| **A: Preserve staging** | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ | ⚠️ | ❌ | 1/5 |\n| **B: Forbid resume** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ⚠️ | ✅ | 4/5 |\n| **C: Deterministic compression** | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | 4/5 |\n\n**Key:**\n- ✅ = Positive impact (fully meets requirement)\n- ⚠️ = Mixed impact (trade-off or caveat)\n- ❌ = Negative impact (violates requirement or high cost)\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Chosen Option: **Option B — Forbid Resume When Compression is Enabled**\n\n**Rationale:**\n\n1. **Privacy preservation (T4 compliance):** No staging persistence → privacy posture unchanged\n2. **Correctness guarantee:** Explicitly disabling unsafe resume prevents silent corruption\n3. **Low implementation complexity:** ~50-100 lines vs. 300-1200 lines for alternatives\n4. **Clear user contract:** Speed vs. robustness trade-off is transparent\n5. **No new maintenance burden:** No custom compressor or session tracking\n\n**Why not Option A (preserve staging)?**\n- Violates T4 privacy requirement (staging must be wiped)\n- Adds significant complexity (session tracking, abandonment UI)\n- Storage exhaustion risk on multi-GB files\n- Cross-platform stability issues remain (staging may not survive OS cleanup)\n\n**Why not Option C (deterministic compression)?**\n- High implementation and maintenance complexity\n- Custom WASM compressor dependency\n- Build pipeline complexity\n- Versioning issues (compressor updates invalidate resume states)\n- `CompressionStream` is fast and well-integrated — replacing it is costly\n\n**Why Option B is acceptable:**\n- Users choose between two valid modes:\n  - **Compression ON:** 3-10× faster transfers, no resume (stable connections)\n  - **Compression OFF:** Slower transfers, resume supported (unstable connections)\n- The trade-off is explicit and understandable\n- Implementation is trivial and low-risk\n- Privacy and correctness are fully preserved\n\n## Implementation Outline\n\n### Phase 1: Beacon Protocol (Priority: P0)\n\n1. **Add `ResumeDisabled` flag to beacon**\n   ```typescript\n   // src/core/frame/beacon.ts\n   export enum BeaconFlags {\n     None = 0,\n     Compressed = 1 << 0,\n     ResumeDisabled = 1 << 1,  // NEW\n   }\n   ```\n\n2. **Set flag when compression enabled**\n   ```typescript\n   // Sender beacon construction\n   if (compressionEnabled) {\n     flags |= BeaconFlags.Compressed | BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled;\n   }\n   ```\n\n3. **Update plan.md §8.3** to document restriction explicitly\n\n### Phase 2: Receiver Resume Logic (Priority: P0)\n\n1. **Check flag before persisting resume state**\n   ```typescript\n   // Receiver beacon handler\n   if ((beacon.flags & BeaconFlags.ResumeDisabled) !== 0) {\n     // Do NOT persist bitmap or metadata\n     showResumeDisabledMessage();\n     return;\n   }\n   ```\n\n2. **Suppress resume UI when flag present**\n   ```typescript\n   // UI rendering\n   const canResume = !isResumeDisabled(beacon.flags) && hasPersistedState();\n   ```\n\n### Phase 3: User Communication (Priority: P1)\n\n1. **Add clear messaging:**\n   - \"Resume is not available when compression is enabled\"\n   - \"To enable resume, disable compression in settings\"\n\n2. **Compression toggle tooltip:**\n   - \"Compression: Faster transfers (3-10×), but cannot resume if interrupted\"\n   - \"No compression: Slower transfers, but can resume from last completed block\"\n\n### Phase 4: Testing (Priority: P0)\n\n1. **Unit tests:**\n   - Test beacon flag setting when compression enabled\n   - Test receiver resume suppression when flag present\n\n2. **Integration tests:**\n   - Compressed transfer interrupted → reload → resume UI NOT shown\n   - Uncompressed transfer interrupted → reload → resume UI shown\n   - Beacon flag validation end-to-end\n\n3. **Property tests:**\n   - For any file, compression produces deterministic blocks within ONE session\n   - For any file, re-compression MAY produce different blocks across sessions\n\n### Phase 5: Documentation (Priority: P1)\n\n1. **Update plan.md:**\n   - §8.3: Explicitly document \"Resume unsupported when compression enabled\"\n   - §12 (T4): Confirm staging lifecycle unchanged\n   - §10 (E11): Confirm reaping behavior unchanged\n\n2. **Update user documentation:**\n   - Compression vs. resume trade-off explanation\n   - When to use each mode\n\n## Status\n\n✅ **Decision made:** Option B (Forbid resume when compression is enabled)  \n✅ **Implementation status:** Partially complete\n  - Beacon flags defined in `src/core/frame/beacon.ts`\n  - `isResumeDisabled()` function implemented\n  - Plan.md §8.3 documents the restriction\n⏳ **Remaining work:**\n  - Receiver resume UI suppression (integration test coverage)\n  - User-facing messaging\n  - Integration test suite completion\n\n## References\n\n- **Investigation:** `docs/notes/bf-2vke-compression-resume-t4-reap-interaction.md`\n- **Resolution umbrella:** `bf-17s0`\n- **Plan.md §8.3:** Resume specification\n- **Plan.md §12 T4a:** Sender-side staging privacy\n- **Plan.md D8:** Compression to staging file\n- **Plan.md E11:** Abandoned staging reaping\n","numLines":308,"startLine":1,"totalLines":308}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_fd4e78cc4a3d49da8aaf1cde","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# screenferry\n2\t\n3\t**Move a file between two devices using nothing but a screen and a camera.**\n4\t\n5\tscreenferry is a static web app — no server, no backend, no network between the two\n6\tdevices. The sending device reads a local file and flashes it out as an animated\n7\tsequence of optical codes. The receiving device runs the *same* web app, points\n8\tits camera at the sender's screen, decodes the frames, and reassembles the\n9\toriginal file byte-for-byte.\n10\t\n11\t```\n12\t┌─────────────────┐                      ┌─────────────────┐\n13\t│    SENDER       │   ░▓█ optical ░▓█    │    RECEIVER     │\n14\t│                 │  ──────────────────► │                 │\n15\t│  file → chunks  │  animated code frames│  frames → file  │\n16\t│  → coded frames │   screen to camera   │  → download     │\n17\t└─────────────────┘                      └─────────────────┘\n18\t        same static web app on both ends\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\tAnimated QR codes are the obvious way to do this, and the likely starting point —\n22\tbut the goal is the *channel*, not the format. QR spends most of its capacity on\n23\trobustness screenferry doesn't need (arbitrary angles, print damage, one bit per\n24\tmodule). Denser modulation — colored cells, stacked RGB codes, a purpose-built\n25\tgrid codec — is on the table wherever it survives a real phone camera. The\n26\tarchitecture keeps that layer swappable.\n27\t\n28\t## Why\n29\t\n30\tThe screen-to-camera channel is the one data path that survives when everything\n31\telse is unavailable or untrusted:\n32\t\n33\t- **Air-gapped systems** — the receiving machine is deliberately not on any network.\n34\t- **Hostile or captive networks** — hotel wifi, conference NAT, corporate DLP.\n35\t- **Cross-ecosystem transfer** — AirDrop doesn't talk to Android; Nearby Share doesn't talk to iOS.\n36\t- **No pairing, no accounts, no cloud** — nothing is uploaded anywhere, because\n37\t  there is nowhere to upload it to. The bytes go from screen to lens.\n38\t- **Provably no exfiltration** — a static page with no network calls can be\n39\t  audited once and trusted thereafter.\n40\t\n41\t## The hard part\n42\t\n43\tThe channel is **strictly one-way**. The sender has no idea how the receiver is\n44\tdoing — there is no ACK, no retransmit request, no back-channel of any kind. The\n45\treceiver *will* miss frames: autofocus hunting, motion blur, glare, the user\n46\tlooking away.\n47\t\n48\tA naive \"loop through chunks 1..N forever\" transfer degrades badly under loss\n49\t(the coupon-collector problem — the last few chunks take agonizingly long).\n50\tscreenferry's design centers on **rateless erasure coding**: the sender emits an\n51\tendless stream of encoded frames, and the receiver reconstructs the file once it\n52\thas collected *any* sufficient subset. Miss a frame and it simply doesn't\n53\tmatter — the next one is just as useful.\n54\t\n55\t## Status\n56\t\n57\t**Research complete. Phase 1 core codec built (22 tests green). Phase 0 harness and Phase 0.5 spike both partial** — see [`docs/plan/plan.md`](docs/plan/plan.md) §17.2.\n58\t\n59\t- [`docs/plan/plan.md`](docs/plan/plan.md) — the complete application plan\n60\t- [`docs/notes/`](docs/notes/) — design decisions and constraints specific to screenferry\n61\t- [`docs/research/`](docs/research/) — findings:\n62\t  - `qr-encoding-capacity.md` — QR versions, EC levels, bytes per frame, JS encoders\n63\t  - `browser-qr-scanning.md` — camera capture, decoder libraries, binary safety\n64\t  - `fountain-codes-and-protocol.md` — erasure coding for a one-way channel, wire format\n65\t  - `beyond-qr-optical-channels.md` — color barcodes, screen-camera research, what beats QR\n66\t  - `custom-codec-engineering.md` — feasibility of a purpose-built codec in a browser\n67\t  - `pwa-platform-and-ux.md` — offline PWA, file I/O, iOS constraints, UX, testing\n68\t\n69\t## Expectations\n70\t\n71\tThis is not a fast pipe. Realistic throughput is on the order of **kilobytes per\n72\tsecond** — good for keys, configs, documents, and small archives; not for video.\n73\tThe app is designed to be honest about this up front rather than let a user start\n74\ta transfer that would take an hour.\n75\t\n76\t## License\n77\t\n78\tMIT\n79\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5a62087b-b371-40a2-aab6-f52ada081cb2","uuid":"15abbc60-b032-4468-96b1-16e7bc62161b","timestamp":"2026-08-02T09:00:51.625Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/coding/screenferry/README.md","content":"# screenferry\n\n**Move a file between two devices using nothing but a screen and a camera.**\n\nscreenferry is a static web app — no server, no backend, no network between the two\ndevices. The sending device reads a local file and flashes it out as an animated\nsequence of optical codes. The receiving device runs the *same* web app, points\nits camera at the sender's screen, decodes the frames, and reassembles the\noriginal file byte-for-byte.\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐                      ┌─────────────────┐\n│    SENDER       │   ░▓█ optical ░▓█    │    RECEIVER     │\n│                 │  ──────────────────► │                 │\n│  file → chunks  │  animated code frames│  frames → file  │\n│  → coded frames │   screen to camera   │  → download     │\n└─────────────────┘                      └─────────────────┘\n        same static web app on both ends\n```\n\nAnimated QR codes are the obvious way to do this, and the likely starting point —\nbut the goal is the *channel*, not the format. QR spends most of its capacity on\nrobustness screenferry doesn't need (arbitrary angles, print damage, one bit per\nmodule). Denser modulation — colored cells, stacked RGB codes, a purpose-built\ngrid codec — is on the table wherever it survives a real phone camera. The\narchitecture keeps that layer swappable.\n\n## Why\n\nThe screen-to-camera channel is the one data path that survives when everything\nelse is unavailable or untrusted:\n\n- **Air-gapped systems** — the receiving machine is deliberately not on any network.\n- **Hostile or captive networks** — hotel wifi, conference NAT, corporate DLP.\n- **Cross-ecosystem transfer** — AirDrop doesn't talk to Android; Nearby Share doesn't talk to iOS.\n- **No pairing, no accounts, no cloud** — nothing is uploaded anywhere, because\n  there is nowhere to upload it to. The bytes go from screen to lens.\n- **Provably no exfiltration** — a static page with no network calls can be\n  audited once and trusted thereafter.\n\n## The hard part\n\nThe channel is **strictly one-way**. The sender has no idea how the receiver is\ndoing — there is no ACK, no retransmit request, no back-channel of any kind. The\nreceiver *will* miss frames: autofocus hunting, motion blur, glare, the user\nlooking away.\n\nA naive \"loop through chunks 1..N forever\" transfer degrades badly under loss\n(the coupon-collector problem — the last few chunks take agonizingly long).\nscreenferry's design centers on **rateless erasure coding**: the sender emits an\nendless stream of encoded frames, and the receiver reconstructs the file once it\nhas collected *any* sufficient subset. Miss a frame and it simply doesn't\nmatter — the next one is just as useful.\n\n## Status\n\n**Research complete. Phase 1 core codec built (22 tests green). Phase 0 harness and Phase 0.5 spike both partial** — see [`docs/plan/plan.md`](docs/plan/plan.md) §17.2.\n\n- [`docs/plan/plan.md`](docs/plan/plan.md) — the complete application plan\n- [`docs/notes/`](docs/notes/) — design decisions and constraints specific to screenferry\n- [`docs/research/`](docs/research/) — findings:\n  - `qr-encoding-capacity.md` — QR versions, EC levels, bytes per frame, JS encoders\n  - `browser-qr-scanning.md` — camera capture, decoder libraries, binary safety\n  - `fountain-codes-and-protocol.md` — erasure coding for a one-way channel, wire format\n  - `beyond-qr-optical-channels.md` — color barcodes, screen-camera research, what beats QR\n  - `custom-codec-engineering.md` — feasibility of a purpose-built codec in a browser\n  - `pwa-platform-and-ux.md` — offline PWA, file I/O, iOS constraints, UX, testing\n\n## Expectations\n\nThis is not a fast pipe. Realistic throughput is on the order of **kilobytes per\nsecond** — good for keys, configs, documents, and small archives; not for video.\nThe app is designed to be honest about this up front rather than let a user start\na transfer that would take an hour.\n\n## License\n\nMIT\n","numLines":79,"startLine":1,"totalLines":79}}}
